[5053] Figure 8: Starter Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5054] The Starter Screen is the first screen users see when they open DriveAlert. It introduces the application as a drowsiness-monitoring companion and provides two options: Sign In for existing users and Create Account for new users.
[5055] 
[5056] 
[5057] Figure 9: Sign In Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5058] The Sign In Screen allows existing users to access their DriveAlert accounts using their email address and password. Users may show or hide the password, recover a forgotten password, or proceed to the Create Account Screen to register.
[5059]   
[5060] 
[5061] Figure 10: Create Account Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5062] The Create Account Screen allows new users to register for DriveAlert. It includes fields for first name, optional middle name, last name, optional phone number, email address, password, and password confirmation. Users can select Create Account to register or Sign in to return to the login screen.
[5063] 
[5064] 
[5065] Figure 11: Forgot Password Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5066] The Forgot Password Screen helps users recover access to their DriveAlert accounts. Users enter the email address associated with their account and select Send Reset Link to request password-reset instructions. The Back to Sign In button returns users to the login screen.
[5067] 
[5068] 
[5069] Figure 12: Setup and Device Screen (Driver)
[5070] The Setup and Device Screen guides the driver through the preparations needed before monitoring. It shows the status of the device connection, camera alignment, and driver calibration. The driver can open each setup item to complete the required checks or select Continue to Home to access the Driver Home Screen.
[5071] 
[5072] 
[5073] Figure 13: Device Connection Screen (Driver)
[5074] The Device Connection Screen allows the driver to find and connect the DriveAlert camera through secure Bluetooth provisioning. The driver can scan for a nearby device and provide hotspot or local Wi-Fi details during setup. Manage device access opens the Subscriptions and Drivers Screen for registration-related access, additional drivers, and invitations.
[5075] 
[5076] 
[5077] Figure 14: Camera Alignment Screen (Driver)
[5078] The Camera Alignment Screen helps the driver position the camera and define the driver area before monitoring. It displays the live camera preview with a blue selection box and controls for adjusting the area. The driver follows the positioning instructions and confirms the alignment while parked.
[5079] 
[5080] 
[5081] Figure 15: Driver Calibration Screen (Driver)
[5082] The Driver Calibration Screen guides the driver through personalized calibration while parked. It displays the live camera preview, driver visibility, facial lighting, and calibration status. The driver follows the guided eye, mouth, and head-position steps after confirming camera alignment.
[5083]   
[5084] 
[5085] Figure 16: Driver Home Screen (Driver)
[5086] The Driver Home Screen summarizes monitoring readiness and recent activity. Before a session, it provides controls to start monitoring, preview the camera, and open Setup and Device. During a session, it shows the active monitoring status and Stop Monitoring control. Recent activity includes alert counts, the latest alert and session, and approved Trusted Contacts.
[5087] 
[5088] 
[5089] Figure 17: Camera Preview Screen (Driver)
[5090] The Camera Preview Screen displays the live video received from the connected DriveAlert camera. The driver can use the preview to check the camera view before returning to Driver Home. The back button returns the driver to the previous screen.
[5091] 
[5092] 
[5093] Figure 18: Before Monitoring Dialog (Driver)
[5094] The Before Monitoring dialog allows the driver to identify conditions that may affect facial visibility for the current session. The available options include no obstruction, ordinary eyeglasses, a face mask, sunglasses, or a mask with sunglasses. Each option explains its effect on monitoring availability. The driver can start monitoring or cancel.
[5095] 
[5096] 
[5097] Figure 19: Monitoring Activity Screen (Driver)
[5098] The Monitoring Activity Screen displays the monitoring status and live camera preview. It shows driver tracking, session duration, the current warning, confirmed events, the latest event, and camera visibility. When monitoring is inactive, the screen directs the driver to start a session from Driver Home.
[5099] 
[5100] 
[5101] Figure 20: Alert History Screen (Driver)
[5102] The Alert History Screen presents saved confirmed warning events and a summary of recent alert activity. It shows alert counts for today and the past seven days, the highest recorded Warning Stage, and related insights. The driver can filter records by Warning Stage. When no records are available, the screen displays the No alert history message.
[5103] 
[5104] 
[5105] Figure 21: Alert Detail Screen (Driver)
[5106] The Alert Detail Screen displays a saved confirmed warning event. It shows the date and time, detected visible sign, monitoring session, warning output, and Warning Stage at detection. The screen also explains the record visibility and Trusted Contact synchronization eligibility. The driver can return to the alert list or delete the record.
[5107] 
[5108] 
[5109] Figure 22: Trusted Contacts Screen (Driver)
[5110] The Trusted Contacts Screen allows the driver to review approved Trusted Contacts, incoming connection requests, and sent requests. Incoming requests display the account name and email address, together with Accept and Decline buttons. The driver can approve a connection, remove an approved contact, or select Invite a Trusted Contact to add another person.
[5111] 
[5112] 
[5113] Figure 23: Invite a Trusted Contact Screen (Driver)
[5114] The Invite a Trusted Contact Screen allows the driver to find another DriveAlert account using its connection code. The driver enters the code displayed on the other person's Account Screen and selects Find account. After the connection is approved, future eligible Stage 3 records can be shared with that Trusted Contact.
[5115] 
[5116] 
[5117] Figure 24: Shared Alerts Screen (Trusted Contact)
[5118] The Shared Alerts Screen allows a Trusted Contact to review eligible Stage 3 records shared by a connected driver. It displays the driver's name, received and unread record counts, and the latest received date and time. The Stage 3 synchronization timeline shows when a warning started, when the record was received, its sharing status, the number of events counted in the period, and the detected visible signs.
[5119] 
[5120] 
[5121] Figure 25: Invite a Driver Screen (Trusted Contact)
[5122] The Invite a Driver Screen allows a Trusted Contact to find a driver's account using its connection code. After an account is found, the screen displays the account name for verification. The user can select Send connection request to invite the driver or Cancel to return. The driver must approve the connection before future eligible Stage 3 records can be shared.
[5123] 
[5124] 
[5125] Figure 26: Requests Screen (Trusted Contact)
[5126] The Requests Screen allows a Trusted Contact to review incoming and sent connection requests. It explains that accepting a driver connects the accounts and allows future eligible Stage 3 transition or persistence records to be shared. When no requests are available, the screen displays separate empty states for incoming and sent requests.
[5127]   
[5128] 
[5129] Figure 27: Driver Settings Screen (Driver)
[5130] The Driver Settings Screen provides access to the driver’s account, device setup, monitoring activity, history, monitoring logs, notifications, and warning sound. Subscriptions and Drivers opens device-access management and invitations for additional drivers. The driver can also switch to Trusted Contact view, read information about DriveAlert, or log out.
[5131]   
[5132] 
[5133] Figure 28: Subscriptions and Drivers Screen (Driver)
[5134] The Subscriptions and Drivers Screen shows the driver’s device access and identifies the primary driver whose access is included with the device. The primary driver can invite additional drivers using their connection codes and refresh access online. The screen explains that subscriptions are simulated, no payment is collected, and each driver’s calibration and alert history remain separate.
[5135] 
[5136] 
[5137] Figure 29: Invite an Additional Driver Screen (Driver)
[5138] The Invite an Additional Driver section allows the primary driver to enter another user’s DriveAlert connection code. Selecting Find Driver looks up the intended account before the primary driver confirms the simulated subscription. The invited driver must accept the invitation before using the device.
[5139] 
[5140] 
[5141] Figure 30: Demo Subscription Activation Dialog (Driver)
[5142] The Demo Subscription Activation dialog displays the selected account and asks the primary driver to confirm additional-driver access for the device. It clearly states that the subscription is simulated and no payment will be collected. Confirm Activation sends the device invitation, while Cancel returns without confirming it.
[5143] 
[5144] 
[5145] Figure 31: Device Invitation Screen (Driver)
[5146] The Device Invitation Screen shows a pending invitation received by an additional driver. It identifies the device and the primary driver who sent the invitation. The recipient may accept or decline while online. Accepted access can be saved for later offline device use, while calibration and alert history remain specific to the driver’s account.
[5147] 
[5148] 
[5149] Figure 32: Active Additional Driver Access Screen (Driver)
[5150] The Active Additional Driver Access Screen shows an accepted account under the primary driver’s additional-driver list with an Active demo subscription status. The primary driver can remove access or invite another driver. Removal preserves saved history; the primary driver must reconnect to the device through Bluetooth to synchronize the removal to the device.
[5151] 
[5152] 
[5153] Figure 33: Trusted Contact Settings Screen (Trusted Contact)
[5154] The Trusted Contact Settings Screen provides access to account and application preferences for Trusted Contact users. It includes options to switch to Driver view, open the account profile, manage history and storage, adjust notifications, and view information about DriveAlert. The user can also log out from this screen.
[5155] 
[5156] 
[5157] Figure 34: Account Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5158] The Account Screen displays the signed-in user's name, email address, and phone information. Users can select Edit profile to update their details. It also shows the account connection code and provides Copy code and Share code options. Connections still require approval before they become active.
[5159] 
[5160] 
[5161] Figure 35: History and Storage Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5162] The History and Storage Screen allows users to choose how long Driver Alert History and Trusted Contact shared history remain on the phone. The available options include one day, three days, seven days, a custom period, or keeping history until it is deleted manually. The screen explains the effect of deletion and provides a Save button for applying changes.
[5163] 
[5164] 
[5165] Figure 36: Monitoring Logs Screen (Driver)
[5166] The Monitoring Logs Screen lists monitoring sessions recorded for the signed-in Driver on the phone. Sessions are grouped by date and show their time and completion status. The driver can open a session to review its timeline, choose automatic deletion settings, or clear completed logs.
[5167] 
[5168] 
[5169] Figure 37: Session Log Screen (Driver)
[5170] The Session Log Screen displays the timeline of a selected monitoring session. It shows the session date, start and end times, detected signs, recorded measurements, and alarm requests. Each alarm entry indicates the response from the ESP32 device. The driver can review the recorded events or delete a completed session log.
[5171] 
[5172] 
[5173] Figure 38: Log Deletion Screen (Driver)
[5174] The Log Deletion Screen controls how long completed monitoring-session logs remain on the phone. The driver can choose one day, three days, seven days, a custom period, or manual deletion. The screen explains that this setting is separate from Alert History and that current sessions are kept. Changes can be applied using Save.
[5175] 
[5176] 
[5177] Figure 39: Driver Notifications Screen (Driver)
[5178] The Driver Notifications Screen allows the driver to choose which DriveAlert updates can appear as Android notifications. It provides separate switches for confirmed warning alerts, setup and device reminders, and contact request updates. Each setting includes a short explanation of the information it controls.
[5179] 
[5180] 
[5181] Figure 40: Trusted Contact Notifications Screen (Trusted Contact)
[5182] The Trusted Contact Notifications Screen allows the user to manage Android notifications related to shared records and connections. It provides switches for connection requests and new shared Stage 3 records. These options control updates about driver connection requests and eligible records received from connected drivers.
[5183] 
[5184] 
[5185] Figure 41: Warning Sound Screen (Driver)
[5186] The Warning Sound Screen allows the driver to choose a predefined warning sound and the preferred device volume. Available sounds include Digital Beep, Rooster Call, Alarm Clock, Digital Beep 2, and Bell Chime. The volume options are Minimum, Medium, and High. Each selection is played through the connected DriveAlert speaker for an immediate preview.
[5187] 
[5188] 
[5189] Figure 42: About DriveAlert Screen (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5190] The About DriveAlert Screen explains the application's purpose, monitored visible signs, Warning Stages, privacy, and limitations. It identifies prolonged eye closure, yawning, and head nodding as the selected signs. The screen also explains that Warning Stages represent increasing system intervention and that approved Trusted Contacts receive only eligible Stage 3 record details.
[5191] 
[5192] 
[5193] Figure 43: Log Out Confirmation Dialog (Driver and Trusted Contact)
[5194] The Log Out Confirmation dialog appears when a user selects Log out from Settings. It asks the user to confirm leaving the signed-in session and explains that session-only changes will be cleared. The user can select Log out to continue or Cancel to remain signed in.
[5195] 
[5196] 
[5197] Figure 44: Sign In Screen (Web)
[5198] The Sign In Screen allows existing users to access the DriveAlert web portal using their email address and password. Users can select Forgot password to recover account access or create a Trusted Contact account through the registration link. After signing in, users can review approved drivers and shared alert records.
[5199] 
[5200] Figure 45: Create Account Screen (Web)
[5201] The Create Account Screen allows new users to register as Trusted Contacts. It includes fields for first name, optional middle name, last name, optional phone number, email address, password, and password confirmation. Users can select Create account to register or Sign in to access an existing account.
[5202] 
[5203] Figure 46: Dashboard Screen (Web)
[5204] The Dashboard Screen summarizes connected drivers, pending requests, shared alerts, and the latest shared activity. It lists recent shared records with their warning and received times, event counts, and detected signs. Users can review requests or invite a driver. The displayed activity represents shared historical records rather than live monitoring.
[5205] 
[5206] Figure 47: Connected Drivers Screen (Web)
[5207] The Connected Drivers Screen lists drivers whose connections have been approved. Each entry displays the driver's name, approval status, connection date, and shared-record activity. Users can select View records to open a driver's shared history, Disconnect to end a connection, or Invite a Driver to begin another connection.
[5208] 
[5209] Figure 48: Shared Alert History Screen (Web)
[5210] The Shared Alert History Screen displays records received from a selected driver. It shows the driver's name, approval status, connection date, and shared-record count. Each history entry includes the warning time, received time, sharing status, event count, and detected signs. Users can open a record, clear the history, or return to Drivers.
[5211] 
[5212] Figure 49: Shared Alert Detail Screen (Web)
[5213] The Shared Alert Detail Screen presents a selected shared record. It includes the driver, record type, period start and end, detected signs, event count, session identifier, and synchronization information. It explains that the event count covers the warning period. Users can delete the record or return to the driver's shared history.
[5214] 
[5215] Figure 50: Requests Screen (Web)
[5216] The Requests Screen separates incoming and outgoing driver connection requests. It explains that accepting a driver allows future shared alerts to be viewed. Empty panels indicate when no requests are available. Users can select Invite a Driver to begin a new connection request.
[5217] 
[5218] Figure 51: Invite a Driver Screen (Web)
[5219] The Invite a Driver Screen allows users to find a driver using the connection code displayed on the driver's Account Screen. Users enter the code and select Find Driver to look up the account. The driver must approve the connection before future shared alerts can be viewed. The Back link returns to the previous screen.
[5220] 
[5221] Figure 52: Account Screen (Web)
[5222] The Account Screen displays the user's full name, email address, phone number, role, and account status. Users can select Edit profile to update their information. The connection-code panel displays the active account code and a Copy code button for use when connecting with a driver.
[5223] 
[5224] Figure 53: Settings Screen (Web)
[5225] The Settings Screen provides access to History and Storage, Account, and About DriveAlert. It also includes a Log out button. The notification area explains that remote notifications are currently unavailable, allowing users to distinguish the portal's supported settings from features that are not available.
[5226] 
[5227] Figure 54: History and Storage Screen (Web)
[5228] The History and Storage Screen lets users choose how long shared history remains visible in the current browser. Options include one day, three days, seven days, a custom period, or manual deletion. Users can save their choice. The screen explains that removals affect only their browser view and do not restore deleted history.
[5229] 
[5230] Figure 55: About DriveAlert Screen (Web)
[5231] The About DriveAlert Screen explains the web portal's purpose, approved access, privacy, and limitations. It describes the sharing of eligible Stage 3 transition or persistence records and the limited information available to Trusted Contacts. It also clarifies that the portal does not provide live camera monitoring, vehicle location, or medical diagnosis.
[5232] 
[5233] Figure 56: Driver Storyboard
[5234] Figure 56 illustrates the DriveAlert Driver workflow. Users sign in or register, then prepare the camera connection, alignment, and calibration before opening Driver Home. The Driver can preview the camera, select a facial-visibility condition, and start or stop monitoring. Alert History presents recorded warnings, while Alert Detail displays the selected event and sharing eligibility. Trusted Contacts allows the Driver to invite contacts and manage connection requests. Driver Settings provides access to Account, Monitoring Activity, History and Storage, Monitoring Logs, Notifications, Warning Sound, and About DriveAlert. Session Log displays recorded session events, while Log Deletion controls completed-log retention. Approved contacts may receive eligible Stage 3 records. The workflow ends with Log Out confirmation and a return to the Starter screen.
[5235] 
[5236] 
[5237] Figure 57: Trusted Contact Storyboard
[5238] Figure 57 illustrates the DriveAlert Trusted Contact mobile workflow. Users sign in or register and enter Trusted Contact view. Connected Drivers displays approved connections or an empty state when no Drivers are connected. Invite a Driver allows account lookup through a connection code, while Requests separates incoming and sent requests. Approval is required before future eligible Stage 3 records can be shared. Selecting an approved Driver opens Shared Alerts, which displays the received warning record, timing, event count, and detected signs. Trusted Contact Settings provides access to Account, History and Storage, Notifications, and About DriveAlert. Users may manage retention and notification preferences, review their connection code, or confirm Log Out to return to the Starter screen.
[5239] 
[5240] 
[5241] 
[5242] 
[5243] 
[5244] 
[5245] Figure 58: Trusted Contact Storyboard (Web)
[5246] Figure 58 illustrates the DriveAlert Trusted Contact web workflow. Users sign in or create an account, then open the Dashboard to review connection and shared-activity summaries. Connected Drivers lists approved Drivers, and View records opens a Driver's shared alert history. Selecting a record displays its warning period, detected signs, event count, and synchronization details. Requests and Invite a Driver support connection management using a Driver's connection code and approval. Settings provides access to Account, History and Storage, and About DriveAlert. Users can review their connection code, choose browser history retention, and read the portal's purpose and limitations. Shared records provide historical awareness. Selecting Log out ends the session and returns the user to Sign In.
[5247] Database Design
[5248] This section refers to the process of creating a detailed data structure for storing, managing, and organizing data in a database management system (DBMS). It involves designing the layout, relationships, and rules that govern how data is stored and accessed within the database.
[5249] 
[5250] Figure 59: Entity Relationship Diagram
[5251] FK denotes logical references across Room and Firestore. Repeated account references remain in the attributes; only declared Room foreign keys are enforced by SQL.
[5252] 
[5253] Figure 59 presents the Entity Relationship Diagram (ERD) of DriveAlert, combining Room’s local monitoring data with Cloud Firestore’s account, sharing, and device-access records. Its 22 entities support personalized calibration, warning history, approved Trusted Contact sharing, and simulated additional-driver subscriptions. FK labels identify logical references; only relationships explicitly declared as Room foreign keys are enforced by SQL.
[5254] User stores account identity, contact details, role, status, registration date, and connection code. The role identifies a Driver, Trusted Contact, or both. ConnectionCode associates an account lookup code with its owner, display name, creation date, and active status. Users enter these codes to request connections or invite additional Drivers.
[5255] DriverPreference stores warning-sound selection, volume, notification preferences, history retention, and update time. MonitoringLogPreference separately stores the Driver’s retention setting for completed diagnostic logs.
[5256] DriverCalibration stores personalized EAR, MAR, and head-pitch thresholds, calibration measurements, detector schema version, date, and active status. A Driver may retain multiple calibration records. MonitoringSession references the calibration used, preserving the relationship between each session and its detection thresholds.
[5257] Device identifies a registered camera by its canonical Bluetooth identifier and claim marker. DeviceOwnership assigns one primary Driver to each device and stores its display name and registration metadata. ProvisionedDevice separately stores each Driver’s saved camera connection, including hostname, network address, stream settings, last connection time, and active status. Its composite key combines driverUserId and deviceId. MonitoringSession records the Driver, device, calibration, timing, status, and highest Warning Stage.
[5258] TrustedContactConnection records the Driver, Trusted Contact, requester, target connection code, status, and request, approval, decline, and revocation times. Drivers and Trusted Contacts may have multiple approved connections. This relationship permits future eligible Stage 3 sharing and is separate from additional-driver device access.
[5259] Alert records confirmed warning events, including detection time, Warning Stage, onboard alarm status, and alarm-trigger time. AlertSign associates each event with prolonged eye closure, yawning, or head nodding. Warning Stages describe increasing system intervention rather than medical drowsiness severity.
[5260] StageSyncRecord stores local Stage 3 transition and persistence records, including session references, warning-period boundaries, event counts, sharing eligibility, and synchronization metadata. StageSyncRecordSign identifies the detected signs. Eligible records are published as SharedStage3Record documents in Cloud Firestore for approved Trusted Contacts. These records provide historical awareness rather than live camera monitoring.
[5261] MonitoringLog and MonitoringLogEvent store diagnostic sessions and their timeline entries, including timing, measured values, thresholds, signs, durations, calibration references, Warning Stages, and alarm-response results. DeviceAccessGrant caches server-verified account access locally in Room, while DeviceIdentityMapping resolves older local identifiers to canonical device identifiers.
[5262] DeviceDriverAccess stores invitations and additional-driver memberships, including their status, activation reference, and acceptance or ending times. DeviceSubscriptionEvent records simulated activations; no real payment is collected. DeviceCredential stores account-specific authorization credentials. An invited Driver accepts and completes setup online, after which cached access and credentials permit offline device use. Each Driver retains separate calibration and alert history. Together, these records support one included primary Driver per device and controlled access for additional Drivers.
[5263] Data Dictionary 
[5264] The data dictionary describes the Room and Cloud Firestore entities used by DriveAlert for accounts, monitoring, alert sharing, and simulated additional-driver subscriptions. PK identifies primary keys and FK identifies logical references; only declared Room foreign keys are enforced by SQL. A dash in Field Size means no fixed length is declared. OPTIONAL fields may be absent, while NULL permits a null value. Subscription activation is simulated and no payment is collected.

[6480] Network Design
[6481] This section includes the planning and implementation of “DriveAlert’s” computer network infrastructure. It involves evaluating, analyzing, and sizing the network to ensure it meets the needs of the application and its users. A well-structured network diagram representing the overall network architecture serves as a roadmap for physically assembling the network.
[6482] Network Model
[6483] A network model is a conceptual framework that describes the architecture, components,
[6484] and interactions within a computer network. It outlines how different devices and services communicate with each other to achieve seamless connectivity and efficient data transfer.

[6509] Figure 60: DriveAlert Network Model
[6510] The network model for “DriveAlert” consists of a DriveAlert IoT device, a driver smartphone, a cloud database or synchronization service, and Trusted Contact access through mobile and web platforms. The IoT device connects directly to the driver’s Android application through local offline Wi-Fi, allowing real-time monitoring and alerting without requiring internet connectivity during active use. When internet or mobile data becomes available, drowsiness-alert records are automatically synchronized to the cloud database. These records can then be accessed by an approved Trusted Contact through the mobile application or web platform, ensuring efficient data flow for drowsiness-alert record synchronization and review.
[6511] Network Topology
[6512] This section presents a visual illustration of the network topology, which represents how devices and components are integrated into a computer network. The topology diagram shows the different links between network nodes and helps in understanding the data flow, addressing mechanisms, and protocols for efficient data transfer.
[6513] 
[6514] 
[6515] 
[6516] 
[6517] 
[6518] 
[6519] 
[6520] 
[6521] 
[6522] 
[6523] 
[6524] 
[6525] 
[6526] Figure 61: DriveAlert Network Topology
[6527] The network topology of “DriveAlert” centers around a cloud-based database and synchronization service, which supports the storage and synchronization of drowsiness-alert records across the system. The platform operates through two main communication paths: a local offline Wi-Fi connection between the DriveAlert IoT device and the driver’s Android smartphone for real-time monitoring and alerting, and an internet or cellular data connection for synchronizing records to the cloud when connectivity is available. Approved Trusted Contacts may then access the synchronized drowsiness-alert records through either a web platform or a mobile device, allowing flexible monitoring and review from different locations. This topology provides both offline functionality for active monitoring and online accessibility for record synchronization, making the system suitable for DriveAlert’s core functions such as drowsiness-sign detection, warning alerts, drowsiness-alert record storage, and Trusted Contact synchronization.

[6574] Table 29 presents the technology stack used to develop DriveAlert. The Android application uses Kotlin, the Android SDK, Jetpack Compose, and Material 3. Room provides local SQLite storage, while WorkManager handles background synchronization. The Trusted Contact web portal uses React, TypeScript, HTML, CSS, Tailwind CSS, and Vite, with Firebase Hosting for deployment.
[6575] Firebase Authentication manages account access, while Cloud Firestore stores cloud records, connections, and simulated additional-driver access. The notification module uses Firebase Cloud Messaging, Cloudflare Workers, and D1 when deployed and configured. MediaPipe Face Landmarker supports facial-landmark detection, while FaceAttribNet runs through TensorFlow Lite for facial-attribute assessment.
[6576] The IoT component uses an ESP32-CAM with an OV3660 camera, C/C++ firmware, and a DFPlayer Mini with a speaker and microSD card for warning audio. BLE supports setup and authorization, while Wi-Fi carries video and warning commands. Android Studio, Gradle, Arduino IDE/CLI, and Git support development, with iOS considered for future expansion.
[6577] Software Specification
[6578] This section presents the software that will be used, like the operating system, programming languages for both the front-end and backend of the application, platforms, and database requirements that will be used to develop the entire system of the DriveAlert. It is similar to creating a blueprint or a set of instructions that researchers must follow to develop the program correctly. Before the construction process can commence, the researchers must create an accurate design that includes the layout of software specifications.

[6598] Table 30 outlines the software specification for developing the “DriveAlert” system. The researchers chose Windows and Android OS Version 8 and above to ensure compatibility with the development environment and supported Android devices. Visual Studio Code and Android Studio are used as the main development tools, while Kotlin, JavaScript, and C/C++ serve as the programming languages for the mobile application, web component, IoT firmware, and AI-related processes. Supported web browsers include Microsoft Edge, Opera, and Google Chrome, allowing users to access the web-based features through commonly used browsers. The mobile platform specified is Android, which supports the driver-side monitoring application. Cloud Firestore serves as the cloud database for synchronized alert records and Trusted Contact access, while SQLite is used for local record storage on the driver’s smartphone. ReactJS is used for building the web interface, while Android Material Design supports a clean and user-friendly mobile interface. These specifications ensure that DriveAlert is developed using suitable tools and technologies to support its core features, including driver drowsiness monitoring, alert record storage, IoT communication, and Trusted Contact synchronization.

[6601] Hardware Specification
[6602] This section offers a comprehensive and detailed description of the hardware's objectives, specifying the exact requirements and constraints for each component essential to fulfill the specifications.
[6603] Table 31

[6627] Table 31 outlines the hardware specifications for “DriveAlert,” detailing the minimum requirements for both personal computers (PCs) and Android mobile devices to support system development and operation. For PC development, the specifications include a CPU with 64-bit or 32-bit support and a minimum clock speed of 2.5 GHz, such as Intel i5 10th generation or AMD Ryzen 5 3000 series and above. A minimum of 8 GB RAM and at least 10 GB of available storage are required, along with integrated or dedicated graphics such as Intel UHD Graphics, AMD Graphics, or Nvidia Graphics. For Android smartphones, the requirements include an octa-core processor with a minimum clock speed of 2.0 GHz, at least 4 GB of RAM, and 6 GB of available storage. These specifications are intended to ensure that DriveAlert can be developed, tested, and operated properly, particularly for mobile-based driver drowsiness monitoring, alert record handling, and communication with the IoT device.
[6628] IoT Architecture
[6629] This section presents how DriveAlert’s hardware components and mobile application connect and work together for monitoring and alerting.
[6630] 
[6631] 
[6632] 
[6633] Figure 62: DriveAlert IoT Hardware Architecture
[6634] Figure 62 presents the updated DriveAlert IoT hardware architecture. The breadboard power module and solderless breadboard distribute operating power and provide a common ground for the ESP32-CAM and DFPlayer Mini. The assembled enclosure contains the camera, warning speaker, and red status LED.
[6635] The ESP32-CAM operates the OV3660 camera and sends its MJPEG video stream to the Driver’s Android smartphone over local Wi-Fi. The smartphone performs facial analysis and monitoring, then sends warning commands and heartbeat messages back to the device. BLE supports setup and device authorization. The red status LED is controlled through GPIO14 and blinks when the application connection is absent.
[6636] For warning output, the ESP32-CAM sends UART commands from GPIO13 to the DFPlayer Mini, which plays recordings stored on its microSD card through the speaker. Once account and device setup are complete, camera streaming, monitoring, and warning output operate locally without internet. Online access supports account and device-access management and synchronization of eligible Stage 3 records for approved Trusted Contacts.
[6637]

[6808] Testing/Quality Assurance Phase 
[6809] The following section presents the application testing performed by the developers to evaluate the performance and functionality of both the mobile and web platforms. The testing process included Unit Testing, Integration Testing, and Alpha/Acceptance Testing to verify that all system components functioned as intended.
[6810] Unit Testing

[7431] 
[7432] Alpha/Acceptance Testing
[7433] This section is conducted to evaluate how the system is judged by the people who use it, based on its performance and functionality from the user’s perspective. It ensures that the system meets the needs of every role and is ready for deployment, with the ratings below filled in by the evaluators, who mark one rating per row.
[7434] Alpha Testing
[7435] Testing is conducted internally by the development team to find and fix problems before release and to confirm that the system works correctly for every type of user.
[7436] Table 35
[7437] ALPHA TESTING
[7438] Test Criteria
[7439] Poor
[7440] Fair
[7441] Good
[7442] Very Good
[7443] Graphical User Interface (GUI)
[7444] Consistency (The user interface is of the same formatting style and icons throughout the system.)
[7445] 
[7446] 
[7447] X
[7448] 
[7449] Reusability (The system contains reusable GUI components such as familiar buttons, text and check boxes, and other tools.)
[7450] 
[7451] 
[7452] X
[7453] 
[7454] Forgiveness and Tolerance (The interface displays message or confirmation prompts that would allow the users to undo or redo critical actions.)
[7455]

[7659] Implementation/Deployment Phase
[7660] This section covers the installation and configuration of DriveAlert for use by Drivers and Trusted Contacts. It includes installing the Android application, uploading the ESP32-CAM firmware, preparing the camera and onboard warning components, configuring Firebase services, and deploying the Trusted Contact web portal through Firebase Hosting. Device registration and initial authorization require an internet connection. Once setup is complete, authorized Drivers can monitor offline while maintaining separate calibration and alert histories. This phase also includes user orientation and verification of camera connectivity, calibration, monitoring, onboard warnings, and eligible Stage 3 record sharing before deployment.
[7661] Deployment Diagram
[7662] This section illustrates how DriveAlert’s components are distributed across devices and network environments. It shows the connections among the ESP32-CAM and its warning components, the Android application that processes camera frames and stores local records, Firebase services, and the Trusted Contact web portal. It also distinguishes local communication used for setup, monitoring, and warnings from internet connections used for account management, device authorization, and shared-record synchronization.
[7663] 
[7664] 
[7665] 
[7666] 
[7667] 
[7668] 
[7669] 
[7670] 
[7671] 
[7672] 
[7673] 
[7674] Figure 63: DriveAlert Deployment Diagram
[7675] The deployment diagram presents DriveAlert’s architecture, showing the connections among the ESP32-CAM device, the Driver’s Android application, cloud services, and the Trusted Contact mobile application and web portal. The ESP32-CAM uses an OV3660 camera to capture frames and transmit them to the Driver’s phone through a local Wi-Fi HTTP/MJPEG connection. BLE supports device setup and authorization. The device also receives warning commands from the phone and plays the selected recordings through the DFPlayer Mini, microSD card, and speaker. After initial online setup, authorized Drivers can use the device offline.
[7676] The Android application, developed using Kotlin and Jetpack Compose, performs vision processing on the phone. MediaPipe Face Landmarker supplies facial landmarks, while personalized calibration and timing rules support the detection of prolonged eye closure, yawning, and head nodding. TensorFlow Lite supports facial-attribute assessment through FaceAttribNet. Room manages local SQLite records, keeping each Driver’s calibration, monitoring sessions, and alert history separate. WorkManager schedules eligible Stage 3 record synchronization and retries when internet connectivity becomes available.
[7677] Firebase Authentication manages sign-in, while Cloud Firestore stores profiles, approved connections, shared records, device ownership, and simulated additional-driver access. Firebase Hosting serves the React and TypeScript Trusted Contact web portal. Approved Trusted Contacts can review shared historical records through the Android application or a web browser. When configured, a Cloudflare Worker and D1 database support notification registration and delivery tracking, while Firebase Cloud Messaging delivers Android push notifications. These internet-based services supplement local monitoring and onboard warnings; camera frames are processed on the Driver’s phone rather than uploaded for cloud analysis.

[7714] 
[7715] Table 37 details DriveAlert’s estimated development and implementation cost of ₱10,500. The hardware allocation includes ₱1,500 for the IoT device parts and ₱5,000 for an Android smartphone. The IoT components include the ESP32-CAM with an OV3660 camera, DFPlayer Mini, speaker, microSD card, and supporting power and wiring components. The camera streams frames to the smartphone through a local Wi-Fi connection, while the DFPlayer Mini and speaker provide audible warnings. The smartphone performs vision processing, applies the Driver’s calibration, and stores monitoring sessions and alert history locally.
[7716] The software and services allocation include ₱4,000 for an AI subscription used during development. Android Studio, Arduino IDE, React, TypeScript, Vite, Git, MediaPipe, and TensorFlow Lite are recorded at no additional cost. Firebase supports authentication, cloud records, and web hosting, while Cloudflare Worker and D1 support the configured Android notification service. These cloud services are also allocated ₱0 for the project’s usage. The simulated additional-driver subscription does not collect real payments and is separate from the AI development subscription.
[7717] Software verification covered account access, Driver-specific history, insights, approved connections, and shared-record synchronization across Android and the web portal. Tests also checked that eligible records remain queued while offline and synchronize when connectivity returns. Physical camera streaming, complete monitoring, and onboard speaker playback still require verification using the assembled device.
[7718] Software Specification
[7719] DriveAlert’s software specifications support its Android application, Trusted Contact web portal, cloud services, and camera-device firmware. The Android application requires Android 8.0 or higher, as defined by its minimum SDK configuration. The web portal is accessed through a browser rather than installed as a desktop application.
[7720] The Android application is developed using Kotlin and Android Studio, with Jetpack Compose and Material 3 providing the interface. Room manages local SQLite records, while WorkManager schedules eligible Stage 3 synchronization and retries. MediaPipe Face Landmarker extracts facial landmarks for calibrated detection rules, while FaceAttribNet runs through TensorFlow Lite for supporting facial-attribute assessment. The Trusted Contact portal uses React, TypeScript, Vite, and Tailwind CSS. Arduino IDE or Arduino CLI supports development of the ESP32-CAM’s C/C++ firmware.
[7721] Firebase Authentication manages sign-in, Cloud Firestore stores cloud records and access relationships, and Firebase Hosting serves the web portal. When configured, Cloudflare Worker, D1, and Firebase Cloud Messaging support Android push notifications. Initial device registration and authorization require internet access, while authorized monitoring can operate offline after setup.
[7722] Hardware Specification
[7723] DriveAlert’s hardware specifications cover the development computer, Android smartphones, and assembled IoT device. The computer supports application development, firmware uploading, and testing. Vision processing runs on the Driver’s smartphone, so a dedicated computer server or desktop graphics card is not required during monitoring.
[7724] For Android deployment, Table 31 specifies an octa-core processor running at approximately 2.0 GHz, at least 4 GB RAM, and 6 GB available storage. These figures represent a proposed testing configuration rather than verified minimum requirements. The phone also needs Wi-Fi and Bluetooth Low Energy for device communication and setup. Internet access supports account services, authorization, and synchronization.
[7725] The IoT device consists of an ESP32-CAM with an OV3660 camera, a DFPlayer Mini, speaker, microSD card containing warning recordings, and supporting power and wiring components. The camera supplies frames to the phone over local Wi-Fi, while the audio components play warning commands received from the application. Each authorized Driver maintains separate calibration and alert history when using the shared device. 
[7726] Human Resource Specification
[7727] This section describes the two types of users in DriveAlert: Drivers and Trusted Contacts. Each role has specific responsibilities and access to the system's features. Drivers manage device setup, monitoring, and personal records, while approved Trusted Contacts review shared historical alerts through the mobile application or web portal.
[7728] Table 38
[7729] HUMAN SPECIFICATION
[7730] Users
[7731] Description
[7732] Driver
[7733] The Driver uses the Android application to connect an authorized DriveAlert device, align the camera, and complete the guided personal calibration while parked. The Driver starts and stops monitoring, previews the camera, receives onboard warnings, and reviews personal alert history, insights, and monitoring logs. The Driver can change warning preferences and manage Trusted Contact requests and approved connections. A primary Driver may also invite additional Drivers through a simulated subscription that collects no real payment. Device registration, invitation acceptance, and initial authorization require internet access. After setup, authorized Drivers can use the device offline while keeping their calibration and alert history separate.
[7734] Trusted Contact
[7735] The Trusted Contact uses the Android application or web portal to request and manage approved connections with Drivers. The Trusted Contact reviews eligible shared Stage 3 transition and persistence records, including their warning periods, detected signs, event counts, and synchronization details. The Trusted Contact can accept or decline requests, disconnect from a Driver, and manage shared-history retention and personal record removal. Shared records become available after an eligible upload and provide historical awareness. Trusted Contact access does not include live camera video, vehicle location, complete Driver alert history, or control of the Driver's monitoring session.
[7736] 
[7737] User Guide
[7738] This section provides instructions for navigating DriveAlert and using its features. It explains the system configuration, user access levels, and the main mobile and web procedures.
[7739] 1. Introduction
[7740] DriveAlert is an IoT-based Driver drowsiness monitoring and alert system using computer vision. Its Android application processes frames from an ESP32-CAM to recognize prolonged eye closure, yawning, and head nodding using the Driver's calibration. Confirmed events produce warning stages and are saved in the Driver's alert history. Approved Trusted Contacts can review eligible Stage 3 records through the mobile application or web portal.
[7741] The Android application uses Kotlin, Jetpack Compose, and Room for local records. Firebase supports accounts, connections, device access, and shared records, while the React and TypeScript web portal serves Trusted Contacts. After initial online setup, authorized Drivers can monitor offline. Eligible shared records synchronize when internet access becomes available.
[7742] 2. System Summary
[7743] 2.1 System Configuration
[7744] The mobile application runs on Android 8.0 or higher. Drivers use Wi-Fi for camera streaming and warning commands, and Bluetooth Low Energy for device setup and authorization. Monitoring requires a connected camera, confirmed alignment, and an active Driver calibration. The phone performs vision processing and stores local records.
[7745] Trusted Contacts can also open https://drivealert.web.app in a web browser. Internet access is required for registration, email verification, connection requests, device invitations, and loading cloud records. Saved local history can be reviewed without reconnecting the camera. Android and browser history preferences are managed separately.
[7746] 2.2 User Access Levels
[7747] DriveAlert provides access according to the selected user view and approved device or Driver connections.
[7748] Driver: Uses the Android application to complete setup, start and stop monitoring, review personal alerts and insights, manage warning preferences, and approve or remove Trusted Contacts.
[7749] Trusted Contact: Uses the Android application or web portal to manage Driver connections and review approved shared historical records. This view does not provide live camera video, vehicle location, or complete Driver history.
[7750] Device access: One primary Driver is included per device. The primary Driver may invite additional Drivers through a simulated subscription without real payment. Additional Drivers must accept online and complete device setup; each Driver keeps separate calibration and alert history.
[7751] 2.3 User Manual
[7752] Mobile
[7753] 1. On the starter screen, tap Create Account to register.
[7754] 2. Enter your first name, last name, email, password, and password confirmation. Middle name and phone number are optional.
[7755] 3. Tap Create Account. Correct any highlighted fields before submitting again.
[7756] 4. Open the verification email and follow its link. Return to DriveAlert and tap I've verified my email.
[7757] 5. Tap Resend verification email if needed, and check your spam folder.
[7758] 6. On Choose your view, tap Continue as Driver or Continue as Trusted Contact.

[7987] 8. For a forgotten password, click Forgot password?, enter your email, and click Send reset link.
[7988] 9. Follow the emailed link, then sign in with the new password. Check your spam folder if the message is missing.

[8075] Installation Guide
[8076] This guide assists Drivers and Trusted Contacts in installing and setting up the DriveAlert Android application.
[8077] 1.  Check Device Requirements
[8078] a.  The smartphone must run Android 8.0 (Oreo) or higher.
[8079] b.  Connect to the internet for registration, email verification, and initial device authorization. Driver setup also requires Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, and the DriveAlert camera device.
[8080] 2.  Install the Application
[8081] a.  Download DriveAlert.apk from the following GitHub release:https://github.com/LhordKent/DriveAlert/releases/tag/v1.0.0
[8082] b.  Open the downloaded APK. If prompted, allow Install unknown apps for the browser or file manager used to open it.
[8083] c.  Tap Install, then Open. If Android offers Update for an existing installation, tap Update.
[8084] 3.  Sign In
[8085] a.  Open DriveAlert. Create an account, or sign in with your registered email and password.
[8086] b.  Follow the verification link sent to your email, then return and tap I've verified my email when requested.
[8087] c.  On Choose your view, select Continue as Driver or Continue as Trusted Contact.
[8088] 4.  Complete Initial Setup
[8089] a.  For Drivers, open Setup & Device and connect the camera. Allow the requested Bluetooth or Nearby devices permissions; older Android versions may require Location permission for scanning.
[8090] b.  While parked, confirm camera alignment and follow the calibration instructions. Return to Home and tap Start Monitoring. After initial online setup, authorized Drivers can monitor offline.
[8091] c.  For Trusted Contacts, connect with a Driver and obtain approval to review eligible shared records. The web portal is also available at https://drivealert.web.app.

[8146] Figure 64: Project Road Map
[8147] Figure 64 presents the DriveAlert project road map, summarizing the activities scheduled from June 25 to November 11, 2026. The road map follows ten two-week sprints and the task dates in the Gantt chart, progressing from the research foundation to final manuscript submission, system deployment, and presentation.
[8148] In June 2026, the team will begin Sprint 1 by defining the research problem, objectives, scope, and study terms. The researchers will prepare the questionnaire, review related literature and studies, and develop the initial drafts of Chapters 1 and 2. These activities establish the basis for the proposed system.
[8149] In July 2026, the team will complete the survey, analyze the research foundation, and revise Chapters 1 and 2. Sprint 2 will complete Chapter 3, workflows, interface designs, database diagrams, and system specifications. Sprint 3 will begin project configuration, schema refinement, IoT component sourcing, and manuscript compliance work.
[8150] In August 2026, the team will finish Sprint 3 and complete the local-storage foundation and remaining compliance activities. Sprint 4 will cover IoT assembly, Bluetooth provisioning, camera streaming, account functions, and simulated additional-Driver access. Sprint 5 will integrate MediaPipe, facial measurements, Driver-area tracking, and lighting and visibility checks.
[8151] In September 2026, Sprint 6 will implement guided calibration, prolonged-eye-closure, yawning, and head-down detection, warning-stage rules, onboard audio, and local records. Sprint 7 will establish approved Trusted Contact connections, eligible Stage 3 records, synchronization retries, and shared mobile and web views. The team will integrate these functions and test the complete workflow.
[8152] In October 2026, Sprint 8 will focus on integrated testing, defect correction, regression checks, and manuscript alignment. Sprint 9 will conduct controlled trials, examine false alerts, missed detections, delays, and warning responses, and analyze the findings. The team will prepare the release-candidate APK and begin Sprint 10 presentation work on October 29.
[8153] In November 2026, the team will complete Sprint 10 by finalizing manuscript corrections, approval requirements, and the submission package. The final manuscript submission, APK installation, firmware upload, and web deployment are scheduled for November 6 to 11. Final verification, presentation preparation, and demonstration rehearsal will conclude the project by November 11.

[8158] 
[8159] CONCLUSION
[8160] Based on the data gathered, the proponents concluded that DriveAlert: An IoT-Based Driver Drowsiness Monitoring and Alert System Using Computer Vision is a beneficial innovation for supporting driver awareness and monitoring visible signs associated with drowsiness. Drivers may experience prolonged eye closure, yawning, and head nodding without immediately recognizing the need to stop and rest. Relying on personal awareness alone may allow these signs to go unnoticed, while the absence of organized alert records limits opportunities for reviewing recurring events and keeping Trusted Contacts informed. These concerns highlight the need for an integrated system that can assist Drivers through monitoring, warning alerts, and record sharing. With DriveAlert, Drivers are provided with a platform that uses an IoT camera and an Android application to monitor selected visible signs through computer vision and personalized calibration. Through local Wi-Fi communication, the application processes camera frames without requiring internet access during monitoring after initial setup and sends warning commands to an onboard speaker. Its dashboard allows Drivers to review alert history and basic insights, while eligible Stage 3 records are synchronized with approved Trusted Contacts through the mobile application or web portal when internet connectivity becomes available. Ultimately, DriveAlert offers a practical approach to combining local monitoring, audible warnings, and approved record sharing, helping Drivers recognize warning events and enabling Trusted Contacts to remain informed while supporting safer driving decisions.

[8172] RECOMMENDATION
[8173] The proponents identified several areas for future improvement and enhancement of the DriveAlert system, such as:
[8174] 1.  Improving the reliability of drowsiness-sign detection by refining Driver calibration, detection thresholds, and temporal-confirmation rules using more diverse Drivers and recorded scenarios, including normal facial movements, different head positions, and facial obstructions, to reduce false alerts and missed detections.
[8175] 2.  Enhancing the IoT hardware by using a more stable regulated power supply, improved camera mounting and enclosure, and suitable infrared illumination to address the prototype's lighting limitations and improve operation under low-light conditions.
[8176] 3. Optimizing video processing, memory use, battery consumption, and temperature management on the Android application, together with broader device-compatibility testing, to improve monitoring stability and warning responsiveness across smartphones with different processing capabilities.
[8177] 4.  Developing an iOS version of the mobile application to support more users while retaining the Driver and Trusted Contact functions, local monitoring, and approved record-sharing features available in the Android system.
[8178] 5.  Expanding system evaluation to include more Drivers, compatible smartphones, and representative vehicle environments, beginning with controlled or simulated conditions, to assess detection reliability, warning response, usability, and continued operation beyond the configurations covered in the study.
[8179] 6.  Strengthening data security by improving the protection of user accounts, device authorization, calibration data, and alert records through stronger access controls, secure local storage, and regular review of cloud security rules to maintain privacy and prevent unauthorized access.
